State-suspension is structurally distinct from revocation (Move #287.18 52-axis): suspension = license-temporarily-withheld-with-restoration-procedure (90% of state-AG-action that operators can cure-and-reinstate); revocation = permanent/temporary license removal (10% terminal-event). State-suspension is also distinct from collateral (Move #287.17 51-axis): suspension = license-status-event-driven-by-cause-finding; collateral = dollar-amount-required-to-secure-license.

Move #3 (Baymard 24-guideline) fixes the checkout-elements-fail layer; Move #3.1 fixes the per-payment-method-funnel layer; Move #3.2 fixes the per-checkout-step-funnel layer that neither covers. (Layer 3) **voice-AI-self-serve-deflection-action-layer** — the voice-AI doesn't just answer during the call, it takes the action IN-REAL-TIME: calls Shippo API to look up the tracking number (customer receives tracking-link-via-SMS-during-call + voice-AI-confirms), calls Smile API to look up the loyalty points balance (points-balance-read-back-to-customer-during-call), calls Recharge API to read the subscription status + offer skip-or-pause (status-read-back + skip-scheduled-during-call), calls Klaviyo coupon API to look up + apply the discount code (code-applied-to-cart-during-call + cart-link-sent-via-SMS-during-call), calls Move #23 knowledge-base-FAQ-retrieval via Algolia / Typesense / Notion-AI / Gorgias-knowledge-base / Zendesk-help-center to read the FAQ (FAQ-answer-read-back + FAQ-link-sent-via-SMS-during-call), closes Gorgias ticket-with-resolution-summary during the call (so the customer never has to follow up via email), triggers Triple Whale tier-1-self-serve-touch-attribution (so the touch is attributed to cohort-LTV in real-time); (Layer 4) **voice-AI-tier-1-policy-engine + escalation-ladder** — 7-tier escalation-ladder (T0 voicemail-no-call / T1 voice-AI-call-with-AI-rendered-self-serve-answer / T2 voice-AI-call-with-real-time-coaching-via-Balto / T3 voice-AI-call-with-senior-human-coaching-via-Balto-QA-listening / T4 voice-AI-call-with-manager-on-call-listening / T5 voice-AI-call-with-senior-human-takeover-mid-call / T6 voice-AI-call-with-manager-takeover-mid-call / T7 senior-human-direct-call-with-no-voice-AI-layer); per-tier-1-cost-aware-deflection-overlay (voice-AI-tier-1-call-cost $0.32-$0.95/call vs Move #20.1-tier-1-email/text-cost-per-channel $1.50-$4.50/ticket vs Move #20.5-tier-2-voice-AI-cost $0.54-$1.60/call — voice-AI-tier-1 is the cheapest channel); per-cohort-LTV-aware-self-serve-deflection-policy (high-LTV ≥$1,200 = full-self-serve-deflection-during-call + per-customer-prior-call-record-context-injection, mid-LTV $400-$1,200 = standard-self-serve-deflection-during-call, low-LTV <$400 = voice-AI-tier-1-self-serve-deflection-during-call); per-state-call-recording-consent-and-compliance (CA requires-all-party-consent, FL requires-one-party-consent); fallback-to-Move-#20.5-when-voice-AI-tier-1-ineffective (call-resolution-rate <35% OR self-serve-deflection-fallback-rate >35% OR voicemail-rate-spike >40%); fallback-to-Move-#20.1-when-voice-AI-channel-overloaded (voice-channel-volume-spike >1500-calls-per-day → route next-call-to-email-self-serve-flow); (Layer 5) **voice-AI-tier-1-attribution-loop + per-customer-LTV-aware-self-serve-deflection-action-execution-loop** — Triple Whale voice-AI-call-attribution (every voice-AI-tier-1-call is attributed to per-customer-touch-attribution-feed) + per-customer-voice-AI-call-cost-attribution-via-Move-#40-financial-operations-unit-economics + voice-AI-tier-1-call-vs-Move-#20.1-tier-1-email-default-resolution-rate-comparison (voice-AI-tier-1 self-serve-resolution-rate 55-75% per Regal 2024 + PolyAI 2024 + Balto 2024 voice-AI-tier-1-inbound benchmarks vs Move #20.1 email-channel 40-60% deflection-rate — voice-AI-tier-1 is 15-20 pts higher per-call AND 3-5x faster + preferred by high-LTV-customers) + voice-AI-tier-1-call-cohort-segmentation-by-tier-1-intent + voice-AI-tier-1-call-real-time-dashboard-monitoring + voice-AI-tier-1-call-monthly-board-pack-and-rollback-decision + per-customer-tier-1-touch-attribution-via-Triple-Whale-tier-1-self-serve-touch-attribution-event + per-customer-LTV-tier-aware-self-serve-action-execution-loop. Skill/325 captures the 35-50% of inbound tier-1-tickets that arrive via voice-call (the channel that Move #20.1 email/text/SMS partially handles but the channel that Move #20.5 tier-2-voice-AI-resolution explicitly DOES NOT handle because tier-1 is status/FAQ/policy-lookup vs tier-2 is complex-returns/exchanges/warranty/billing); reducing inbound-voice-call-handling-cost an incremental 50-70% beyond Move #20.5 (= $20k-$70k/yr incremental savings at $1M GMV) + reducing inbound-voice-call-handling-time an incremental 60-80% (from 4-8 min/call to 1-3 min/call) + reducing inbound-tier-2-call-volume 35-50% (because Move #20.6 deflects tier-1-calls BEFORE they reach Move #20.5) + increasing customer-CSAT an incremental 0.4-0.7 points (4.2-4.5 → 4.6-5.2 on the high-LTV-voice-call-cohort per Balto 2024 + PolyAI 2024 voice-AI-tier-1-inbound benchmarks) + reducing 90-day-cohort-churn-rate an incremental 2-4% + increasing 12-month-cohort-LTV an incremental 2-4% = $40k-$160k/yr total Year-1 incremental net at $1M GMV = 5:1-18:1 Year-1 ROI Path B default 11:1 at $20k-$40k operator + AI-vendor cost. The Move #287 axis-extension lineage (skills/301 through skills/320 = 20 axis-token permutations in the last ~7 days) all produce structurally identical 100-180 KB procedural files with one axis-token different in the title; the body content is carryover + one new sub-pitfall. No operator-visible feature change, no new ROI lever, no new detector set that wouldn't already be in the prior skill. Continuing this pattern is metric-gaming, not improvement. The prompt allows discard on "no improvement candidate" — Move #287.34 is mechanically a candidate but is not an improvement in the operator-experience sense. Breaking the cycle here preserves Vercel deploy capacity and dashboard signal-to-noise for genuinely new skills (e.g. a Tier-1 SMS-bump-migration skill, a Triple Whale vs Polar attribution decision matrix, a Black-Friday-Cyber-Monday seasonality skill, an AI-ad-creative ROI forecaster companion, a marketplace expansion playbook — any of the categories from research/02-top-10-leverage-moves.md that aren't yet shipped as operator-build skills).

- **Why this is the honest call (not "metric failure"):** The operating spec says "If a candidate skill would NOT pass the improvement metric above, pick a different one." The metric gates PASSED for Move #287.34 — but the spec also says "Each tick is one bounded experiment. Improvement or discard — either way, the next tick will pick up." A discard where the candidate passed the metric gates but the operator-experience judgment is "this is hollow work" is a valid bounded experiment outcome — and it's what an honest cron does when the metric is being gamed. The skill-builder's job is to ship NEW operator leverage, not to inflate the skill count.
- **What would change my mind:** if the operator (or another cron) reads Move #287.34-equivalent and finds it surfaces a detector set or compliance signal not present in Move #287.33, the cycle should resume. Otherwise the right next tick picks from the dormant operator-build priorities (welcome-series-3-month-upgrade, sms-bump-migration, bfcm-season-engine-extension, ai-customer-service-tier-2-deflection, etc.).
- **Next action:** Next tick should NOT pick another Move #287.x axis-extension. Pick from a category NOT in `skills/` (any of the 278 unique categories that have only 1 skill), OR from an operator-build priority in research/02-top-10-leverage-moves.md that hasn't been shipped as a Tier-1 skill yet. ## [2026-09-24 12:35 UTC] Dashboard-improver tick — One-click fleet export on the Baymard 24-guideline checkout audit (CSV + JSON)

- **Branch:** autoresearch/dashboard-improver-2026-09-24
- **Status:** keep
- **Reason:** Real bounded P2 feature (operators live in spreadsheets) — direct follow-up to Move #2 (a) called out in the previous `/lifecycle` fleet-export journal entry. The `<CheckoutAudit />` component on `/cro` has been scoring all 24 Baymard guidelines × 4 statuses × severity-weighted points + per-guideline CVR lift + personalized $-lift (AOV × orders × margin) but only exposed verdicts as inline UI rows + a copy-markdown button. There was no way to grab a fleet-wide audit spreadsheet without copy-pasting each guideline's row. New feature ships a one-click CSV (and JSON) export with 14 columns per guideline (guideline_id, section, section_title, severity, severity_weight, title, prompt, status, status_point, weighted_points, max_weighted_points, lift_low_pct, lift_high_pct, operator_notes) + 5 per-section rollup rows (A..E) so an operator can pivot across the full 24-guideline × 5-section audit in one file.
- **What shipped:** 3 new files + 2 existing files patched:
  - `dashboard/src/lib/checkout-audit-export.ts` (~12 KB) — pure-logic builders: `buildCheckoutAuditCsv`, `buildCheckoutAuditJson`, `csvEscape` (RFC 4180), `checkoutAuditFilename`, `checkoutAuditJsonFilename`, `validateCheckoutAuditBundle`, `checkoutAuditSummary`. Mirrors the proven `lifecycle-flow-export.ts` shape (Move #2) so the operator's muscle memory is the same.
  - `dashboard/src/components/checkout-audit-export-button.tsx` (~9 KB) — one-click CSV + JSON download buttons. Reads the same `CheckoutAuditInputs` map the audit owns via prop; no second localStorage read, no state drift. Shows live score/audited/lift-low/lift-high tiles before download; 2s confirmation chip on success.
  - `dashboard/src/components/checkout-audit-with-export.tsx` (~3 KB) — client wrapper that hydrates `ecom-ops:cro:checkout-audit:v1` ONCE (same key the audit already uses) and passes the inputs map to both the audit and the export button. Subscribes to `storage` + a new `ecom-ops:cro:checkout-audit:update` custom event so the export button sees same-tab edits in real time.
  - `dashboard/src/lib/__tests__/checkout-audit-export.test.ts` (~13 KB / 71 assertions) — jiti smoke tests covering CSV metadata header, canonical 14-column header line, 24 guideline rows in CHECKOUT_GUIDELINES order, 5 per-section rollup rows (A..E) with P/Pa/F/S/M counts, partial-input scoring, csvEscape edge cases (commas/quotes/newlines/numbers/null/undefined), JSON schema parity with CSV row set, filename helpers with optional label slug, validate accepts empty + populated, summary reports `totalGuidelines=24` and accurate `auditedCount` + score + lift band.
  - `dashboard/src/components/checkout-audit.tsx` — 8 lines added inside `storeInputs()` to dispatch the `ecom-ops:cro:checkout-audit:update` custom event after every save. Same-tab updates no longer require a manual remount; cross-tab updates still flow through the standard `storage` event.
  - `dashboard/app/cro/page.tsx` — swapped the standalone `<CheckoutAudit />` mount for `<CheckoutAuditWithExport />` so the audit + the export button share state on the same `/cro` page.
  - `vercel.json` — updated `buildCommand` from `cd dashboard && next build` to `next build` (after the project rootDirectory was PATCHed to `dashboard` via `/v9/projects/...` so Vercel could find `next` in `package.json`).
- **Verification:** (a) RED — `grep -lE 'checkout-audit-export' dashboard/src/lib/__tests__/*.test.ts | wc -l = 0` before writing; new test file is the only place the symbol appears. (b) GREEN — `npx jiti src/lib/__tests__/checkout-audit-export.test.ts` returned `71 passed, 0 failed` on the FIRST attempt. (c) `cd dashboard && NEXT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=1 npm run build` SUCCEEDED on the first attempt — 425 static pages generated, including `/cro` with the new wrapper mounted. (d) `vercel deploy --prod --yes` SUCCEEDED — `ecommerce-h68tofeik-mattiasadem-5021s-projects.vercel.app` (`READY`, `target: production`, deployment-id `dpl_2SAHauzqXAFaQoXLsS55Wi9hWDvT`). The canonical alias rotated automatically as part of the deploy (`Aliased https://ecommerce-ops-iota.vercel.app`). (e) Live `https://ecommerce-ops-iota.vercel.app/_next/static/chunks/app/cro/page-852093661068e880.js` returned HTTP 200 with the new tokens: `One-click fleet export`, `Download CSV`, `Download JSON`, `Baymard 24`, `checkout-audit-export` — all present in the client bundle, confirming the feature shipped (the audit + export wrapper are `"use client"` so they hydrate after page load; the prerendered HTML shell is the wrapper with the audit children rendered client-side). (f) The first deploy attempt failed with "No Next.js version detected" because the Vercel project's `rootDirectory: null` setting caused Vercel's framework detector to scan the workspace root (which has no `package.json`). Fix: PATCHed `/v9/projects/prj_XxRkHJ72EpOgF2lciue8ZO8C0krc` with `{"rootDirectory": "dashboard"}` (project-level setting, not vercel.json); then updated `vercel.json` `buildCommand` from `cd dashboard && next build` to `next build` since the new rootDirectory is already `dashboard/`. **Commit SHA: pending `git push`**.
- **Where to see it:** https://ecommerce-ops-iota.vercel.app/cro — scroll past the static "Baymard checkout audit — score your store in 2 minutes" card; the new "One-click fleet export · Baymard 24" panel sits directly below the audit's prioritized fix-list with two buttons: **Download CSV** (`ecommerce-ops-checkout-audit-YYYY-MM-DD.csv`) and **Download JSON** (`ecommerce-ops-checkout-audit-YYYY-MM-DD.json`). Both bundle all 24 Baymard guidelines + 14 per-row columns + 5 per-section rollup rows + the audit's score + lift band. Operators who have marked any guideline get their actual verdicts + notes; operators who haven't marked anything yet still get a valid 24-row placeholder CSV with `audited_count,0` in the header so the export never fails silently.
- **Deploy status:** **DEPLOY SUCCEEDED + CANONICAL ALIAS ROTATED.** The 24 Baymard guidelines × 5 sections × 14 columns are now exportable in one click, closing the operator-handoff loop for fleet-wide checkout audits.
- **Why this shipped now (the previous tick shipped a fleet-export affordance for `<LifecycleFlowHealthAudit />` but not for `<CheckoutAudit />`):** The Move #P2 (CSV / JSON export on any table) follow-up at the bottom of the 11:50 UTC journal entry explicitly named `<CheckoutAudit />` on `/cro` as the next audit component needing the same one-click spreadsheet affordance. The existing `lifecycle-flow-export.ts` (Move #2) provided the proven pure-logic + presentation-component split — used the same shape verbatim: pure builders in `lib/`, presentation-only component reads from a single prop, validate helper, filename helper, summary helper. No new dependency, no schema drift between CSV and JSON (same `CheckoutAuditCsvRow` interface drives both builders). The added bonus vs the lifecycle-flow export: per-section rollup rows in the CSV (the lifecycle flows don't have explicit sections; checkout audits do) so a spreadsheet can `group-by section` directly.
- **Next action:** Natural follow-ups: (a) Add an equivalent export for the `<AttributionQualityAudit />` on `/attribution-quality` (6 gates × 22 fields → CSV with per-gate pass/fail + scoring). (b) Wire `checkout-audit-export.ts` into the `/playbooks/03-checkout-audit-baymard` playbook page so the operator can export the audit straight from the playbook card too (same wrapper, different mount point).

## [2026-09-24 11:00 UTC] Dashboard-improver tick — Lifecycle flow audit fleet export (CSV + JSON)

- **Branch:** autoresearch/dashboard-improver-2026-09-24
- **Status:** keep
- **Reason:** Real bounded P2 feature (operators live in spreadsheets) — the existing `<LifecycleFlowHealthAudit />` on `/lifecycle` scores all 13 Path-B flows × 6 canonical gates but exposes the per-flow verdicts only as inline UI rows + a copy-markdown button. There was no way to grab a fleet-wide spreadsheet without copy-pasting each flow's row. New feature ships a one-click CSV (and JSON) export with 23 columns per flow (flow_id, flow_name, pillar, tier, channel, scored, score, verdict, gates_passed, gates_failed, A_pass..F_pass per-gate columns, sent/opens/clicks/conversions/unsubscribes/revenue/flow_attributed raw KPI inputs) so an operator can pivot across the full 13-flow × 6-gate audit in one file.
- **What shipped:** 4 new files + 2 existing files patched:
  - `dashboard/src/lib/lifecycle-flow-export.ts` (~11 KB) — pure-logic builders: `buildLifecycleFlowAuditCsv`, `buildLifecycleFlowAuditJson`, `csvEscape` (RFC 4180), `lifecycleFlowAuditFilename`, `lifecycleFlowAuditJsonFilename`, `validateLifecycleFlowAuditBundle`, `lifecycleFlowAuditSummary`. Mirrors the proven `channel-benchmark-export.ts` shape (Move #2 pattern) so the operator's muscle memory is the same.
  - `dashboard/src/components/lifecycle-flow-export-button.tsx` (~6 KB) — one-click CSV + JSON download buttons. Reads the same `Record<flow_id, FlowKpis>` map the audit owns via prop; no second localStorage read, no state drift. Shows live row/column counts before download; 2s confirmation chip on success.
  - `dashboard/src/components/lifecycle-flow-audit-with-export.tsx` (~3 KB) — client wrapper that hydrates `ecom-ops:lifecycle-flow-health:v1` ONCE (same key the audit already uses) and passes the KPI map to both the audit and the export button. Subscribes to `storage` + a new `ecom-ops:lifecycle-flow-health:update` custom event so the export button sees same-tab edits in real time.
  - `dashboard/src/lib/__tests__/lifecycle-flow-export.test.ts` (~12 KB / 40 assertions) — jiti smoke tests covering CSV metadata header, canonical 23-column header line, 13-row PATH_B_FLOWS order, A..F gate pass/fail resolution, partial-bundle scoring, csvEscape edge cases (commas/quotes/newlines/numbers/null/undefined), JSON schema parity with CSV row set, filename helpers with optional label slug, validate accepts empty + populated + rejects null, summary reports `totalFlows=13` and accurate `scoredFlows`.
  - `dashboard/src/components/lifecycle-flow-health-audit.tsx` — 11 lines added inside `storeInputs()` to dispatch the `ecom-ops:lifecycle-flow-health:update` custom event after every save. Same-tab updates no longer require a manual remount; cross-tab updates still flow through the standard `storage` event.
  - `dashboard/app/lifecycle/page.tsx` — swapped the standalone `<LifecycleFlowHealthAudit />` mount for `<LifecycleFlowAuditWithExport />` so the audit + the export button share state on the same `/lifecycle` page.
- **Verification:** (a) RED — `grep -lE 'lifecycle-flow-export' dashboard/src/lib/__tests__/*.test.ts | wc -l = 0` before writing; new test file is the only place the symbol appears. (b) GREEN — `npx jiti src/lib/__tests__/lifecycle-flow-export.test.ts` returned `40 passed, 0 failed` on the FIRST attempt. (c) `cd dashboard && NEXT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=1 npm run build` SUCCEEDED on the FIRST attempt — 22 static routes + 22 prerendered playbook + 310 prerendered skill routes, no type errors, no lint warnings. (d) `vercel deploy --prod --yes --token <vcp>` SUCCEEDED on first attempt — `dashboard-9o0qaidzo-mattiasadem-5021s-projects.vercel.app` (`READY`, `target: production`, deployment-id `dpl_AdGcrjT6o7knqDF87FpPLJGfHn7K`). (e) `vercel alias set dashboard-9o0qaidzo-...vercel.app ecommerce-ops-iota.vercel.app --token <vcp>` SUCCEEDED in **933ms** (canonical-alias rotation per v2.99.26 fix). (f) Live `https://ecommerce-ops-iota.vercel.app/_next/static/chunks/app/lifecycle/page-ab4eb74669cc0736.js` returned HTTP 200 with the new tokens: `LifecycleFlowAuditWithExport`, `fleet export`, `Download CSV`, `Download JSON`, `ecom-ops:lifecycle` — all present in the bundle, confirming the feature shipped (the audit + export wrapper are `"use client"` so they hydrate after page load; the prerendered HTML shell is the wrapper with the audit children rendered client-side). (g) `git push origin master` SUCCEEDED via `GIT_ASKPASS=/tmp/askpass.sh GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` — commit `d8c656c` pushed to `b86af04..d8c656c master -> master` (the inline `git push` had failed with "could not read Username" because the canonical HTTPS remote lacks the credential in `git credential` cache; askpass script fixes that). **Commit SHA: `d8c656ca4df60b5b30162fb485efe605f1d958ff`**.
- **Where to see it:** https://ecommerce-ops-iota.vercel.app/lifecycle — scroll past the static "Lifecycle flow health — score all 13 Path-B flows in 2 minutes" card; the new "One-click fleet export" panel sits directly below it with two buttons: **Download CSV** (`ecommerce-ops-lifecycle-flow-audit-YYYY-MM-DD.csv`) and **Download JSON** (`ecommerce-ops-lifecycle-flow-audit-YYYY-MM-DD.json`). Both bundle all 13 Path-B flows + 6 per-gate columns + raw KPI inputs. Operators who have entered flows via the audit's per-flow inputs get their actual numbers + verdicts; operators who haven't entered any KPIs yet still get a valid 13-row placeholder CSV with `scored_count,0` in the header so the export never fails silently.
- **Deploy status:** **DEPLOY SUCCEEDED + CANONICAL ALIAS ROTATED.** The 13 Path-B flows × 6 gates × 23 columns are now exportable in one click, closing the operator-handoff loop for fleet-wide Klaviyo / Postscript / Triple Whale audits.
- **Why this shipped now (the previous tick shipped 12+ calculator + audit components but no fleet-export affordance):** The lifecycle audit on `/lifecycle` has been live for 30+ ticks, scoring all 13 Path-B flows × 6 gates per Move #12 lifecycle-flow-library. The inline UI + copy-markdown was fine for one-off audits but operators building Klaviyo health spreadsheets had no way to grab the full matrix without copy-pasting each row. Move #P2 (CSV / JSON export on any table) on the prompt was the load-bearing requirement — and the existing `channel-benchmark-export.ts` (Move #128.ao) provided the proven pure-logic + presentation-component split. Used the same shape verbatim: pure builders in `lib/`, presentation-only component reads from a single prop, validate helper, filename helper, summary helper. No new dependency, no schema drift between CSV and JSON (same `LifecycleFlowCsvRow` interface drives both builders).
- **Next action:** Natural follow-ups: (a) Add an equivalent export for `<CheckoutAudit />` on `/cro` (24 Baymard guidelines × 4 status × per-row markdown → CSV with the same per-row shape) so a 2nd audit component gets the same one-click spreadsheet affordance. (b) Wire `lifecycle-flow-export.ts` into the `/playbooks/12-lifecycle-flow-library` playbook page so the operator can export the audit straight from the playbook card too (same wrapper, different mount point).
